# FROZEN SECTION

*Produced by Claude.ai. Not modifiable by Claude Code outside a Claude.ai round-trip (cf. § Accepted deviations).*

## Context

This milestone extends the Etch S3/S4/S5 subset to the **full EBNF v0.6 grammar** and advances criterion **C0.2** (Etch usable end-to-end). The parser stays **recursive-descent + Pratt**; the hybrid LR(1) incremental rewrite is Phase 1 (C1.11, `etch-parser.md §23`) and is out of scope. This is the largest Phase 0 milestone — it is delivered in **seven reviewed stages (E1–E7)**, each landing a testable vertical slice, on the M0.5/M0.6/M0.7 model.

**What S3/S4/S5 actually delivered (verify in code before scoping each stage).** The S3 subset is narrow: `component`, `resource`, `rule`, the `when` clause, and basic arithmetic expressions only. The infrastructure delivered is reusable as-is and is what this milestone *extends*: a UTF-8 lexer, a tabular SoA AST (`MultiArrayList(Item|Stmt|Expr|TypeNode)`), a two-pass type-checker, typed diagnostics (`src/etch/`, ~2050 lines, public API in `src/etch/root.zig`), a tree-walking interpreter with a dynamic ECS bridge (runtime component registry, dynamic SoA archetype, resource store), and a **direct AST→Zig codegen with no HIR** (`comptime_query.query(world, .{T...})`, one non-generic function per rule, `Registry.registerAlias`). **`fn`, `struct`, `enum`, `trait`, `impl`, `event`, `tags` are NOT in the delivered subset — they are net-new construct support in this milestone, not completion of existing code.** The "extension" is of the *infrastructure*; most constructs themselves are new. Claude Code confirms the delivered surface (read `src/etch/root.zig` + the S3/S4/S5 corpus) before scoping each stage; it does not re-spec from this brief what already exists.

**Three construct support levels (governing distinction — do not collapse it).** The plan says "interpreter executes all constructs", which is unrealistic as stated: real Tier 1 modules (Cortex, UI/Islandz, Kinesis, Pulse, Ember, Sequencer, Render) are out of Phase 0 scope beyond their interface stub. One cannot *execute* an `audio_graph` or an `anim_graph` without an audio/animation engine behind it. The dependency of Level B constructs on Tier 1 modules is a deliberate architectural property, not a gap. Hence three levels:

- **Level A — real behavioural execution.** `fn`, `component`, `resource`, `event`, `rule`, `tags`, control flow, expressions. The interpreter genuinely executes against the mini-ECS; the codegen emits functional Zig; **the byte-exact differential is the world state after N ticks.**
- **Level B — declaration + internal IR/descriptor, no module wiring.** `behavior`, `routine`, `quest`, `dialogue`, `ability`, `data`, `widget`, `theme`, `motion`, `locale`, `input_mapping`, `shader`, `effect`, `anim_graph`, `audio_graph`, `audio_score`, `sequence`. Full sub-grammar parse + semantic validation + structure construction. The differential is on the **serialized internal IR/descriptor**, not on absent module behaviour.
- **Level C — serialization.** `scene`, `prefab`. Parse + typed extensions (`.scene.etch` / `.prefab.etch`, `E0858`) + validation. Runtime instantiation is M0.9 (`engine-scene-serialization.md`), not this milestone.

Consequence for the 500-line reference differential: the file is built so the diffed observable behaviour comes from **Level A** (rules/events/ECS state). Level B/C constructs appear in it to validate parse + codegen-compiles, not for a behavioural diff.

## Scope

The work is delivered as **seven vertical stages** (parser→interpreter→codegen per construct group), in dependency order. Each stage lands a testable slice and ends with a review gate (`Review Ei`): Claude Code completes the stage, pushes, and waits for a Claude.ai review relayed by Guy before starting the next stage. The stage decomposition is the work breakdown for Step 3 of the execution protocol; it is not a re-scope mechanism and Claude Code never re-cuts it mid-milestone.

**Execution grain inside a stage (fixed — do not ask how to sequence it).** Claude Code implements the entire `Ei` block in a single pass, then stops at the `Review Ei` gate and presents the stage diff. There is no per-construct sub-decomposition and no intra-stage checkpoint: a stage is reviewed once, at its end, on its differential — not feature by feature. Claude Code does not ask Guy how to run a stage; the grain is settled here.

**E1 — Language foundations (non-ECS) + 5 S3 debts + breaking interface + EBNF harness mechanism**
- v0.6 expression/control-flow foundations: exhaustive tiered `match` (Maranget algorithm), closures `|a| e`, `loop` / labeled loops / `break label value`, ranges `..=`, collections `T[N]` / `T[]` / `Map` / `Set` / `Iterator`, casts `as`, error handling `throws` / `throw` / `try` / `catch` / `assert`, `type` aliases. Exercised inside `rule` bodies (delivered at S3). Full stack: parse + interpret + codegen.
- The 5 S3 debts (all lexer/parser/resolver core): **D-S3-trivia** (trivia/doc comments attached to nodes via `TriviaMap`); **D-S3-doccomment** (`///` lexed as doc comment, not line comment); **D-S3-annot-applicability** (annotation applicability validated, cf. `etch-resolver-types.md §13`); **D-S3-annot-field-access** (`@requires(self.health)` parses, fix `continuePostfixAndBinary` → `parsePostfix`); **D-S3-resource-receiver** (`get(T)` / `get_mut(T)` resources without receiver supported in rule body).
- Breaking interface change: `ParseResult.diagnostic: ?Diagnostic` → `diagnostics: []Diagnostic`, plus the top-level recovery sync-point (after a diagnostic, advance to the next top-level keyword or EOF, then resume `parseTopLevel`). Landed here because every later stage consumes this interface.
- EBNF harness mechanism: the `@embedFile` + feed-to-parser machinery for `ebnf_examples_test.zig`, then fed continuously as constructs land.
- **Review E1:** foundations differential (expressions, `match`, closures, error handling) + recovery sync-point test + the 5 S3 debts each with a test.

**E2 — Type-declaration layer (Level A)**
- `fn` (top-level + associated), `struct`, `enum`, `trait`, `impl`. Net-new (the S3 subset lacks them — verify, do not re-spec the existing). Full stack: parse + interpret + codegen.
- Generic syntax `<T>` is **parsed** and **interpreted** (the tree-walker monomorphises at runtime trivially). **Generic codegen monomorphisation is out of scope** (HIR-dependent, Phase 2 — see Out-of-scope). `async fn` syntax is **parsed** here; its interpretation and codegen boundary are set in E3.
- **Review E2:** differential on `fn` / `struct` / `enum` / `trait` / `impl` behaviour; generic instances validated through the interpreter only.

**E3 — Executable ECS layer (Level A complete) + 5 S4 debts + 2 S5 build debts**
- `event` declarations + observers + ECS event bridge.
- `tags` hierarchical declaration: compact bitfield encoding (`etch-validation-ecs.md §5`), operators `has_tag` / `has_no_tag` / `has_any_tag` / `has_all_tags` / `has_no_tags`, `add_tag()` / `remove_tag()` deferred structural mutations at the tick boundary.
- `rule` extended to complex queries with `With<T>` / `Without<T>` / `Changed<T>` filters (`etch-validation-ecs.md §12`).
- Async **interpretation** (the tree-walker is its own runtime: suspend on `await`, resume on wake). Async **codegen is out of scope** (see Out-of-scope).
- Absorb the 5 S4 debts: **D-S4-runtime-query** (`RuntimeQuery` / `world.query_dynamic` consumed on the interpreter hot path, or the abstraction dropped); **D-S4-runtime-report** (`RuntimeReport.last_error` assigned by `execBody`, typed `RuntimeError`/`SourceSpan` propagated to the caller); **D-S4-ecs-bridge-panic** (`ecs_bridge.writeValueAsBytes` returns a `TypeMismatch` variant instead of `@panic`); **D-S4-or-archetype** (`or` predicate gets a bitset short-cut above an archetype-count heuristic); **D-S4-multifilter** (per-rule field filter supports several `has_with_filter` — array, not single).
- Absorb the 2 S5 build debts: **D-S5-etchcook-inproc** (`etch_cook` consumed as an in-process build helper, not a subprocess); **D-S5-synth100-proper** (`synth_100/build.zig` is a proper sub-project depending on `weld_core`, not a placeholder).
- **Review E3:** byte-exact ECS differential (events, observers, filtered queries, tags) + the 7 debts each with a test. **End of E3 = Level A complete in both interpreter and codegen** (async excluded from codegen).

**E4 — Level B gameplay** (6 constructs)
- `behavior`, `routine`, `quest`, `dialogue`, `ability`, `data`: exhaustive sub-grammar parse + semantic validation (`etch-validation-ecs.md`, relevant gameplay sections) + internal IR/descriptor + interpreter builds the structure + codegen emits the Zig structure.
- **Review E4:** internal-IR differential.

**E5 — Level B presentation** (5 constructs)
- `widget`, `theme`, `motion`, `locale`, `input_mapping`: exhaustive parse + validation (`etch-validation-ecs.md`, UI + input sections) + internal IR + interpreter/codegen structure. `input_mapping` is Level B strict: parse + validation + IR, **no input execution**.
- **Review E5:** internal-IR differential.

**E6 — Level B render / animation / audio / cinematic** (6 constructs)
- `shader`, `effect`, `anim_graph`, `audio_graph`, `audio_score`, `sequence`: exhaustive parse + validation (`etch-validation-ecs.md`, render/anim/audio sections) + internal IR + interpreter/codegen structure. `shader` includes the resolver's shader mode (rejects heap/string/Entity/closures/async). SPIR-V/MSL/DXIL/WGSL emission is out of scope (Phase 2+).
- **Review E6:** internal-IR differential.

**E7 — Level C + closure**
- `scene`, `prefab` (Level C): parse + typed-extension enforcement (`.scene.etch` / `.prefab.etch` via `E0858`) + validation.
- **AST stable interface freeze:** audit `src/etch/ast.zig`, document the Level-1 stable surface in `etch-parser.md §10.3` ("Interface contract stable cross-phase"), test via `tests/etch/ast_stable_interface.zig` exercising ≥20 distinct public entry points (NodeKind/ExprKind/StmtKind discriminants, span helpers, intern tables, public iterators). Placed here because all `NodeKind`/`ExprKind` variants are then settled.
- `tests/etch/reference_500_lines.etch` (500+ lines, mix of all constructs), parsed < 50 ms, with a global differential: Level-A behaviour byte-exact interpreter↔codegen; Level-B/C constructs present to validate parse + codegen-compiles, not for a behavioural diff; generic/async fragments present for parse+interpret validation only (no codegen diff).
- Interpreter hot-reload (edit → AST swap) < 500 ms.
- EBNF harness validated: ≥30 Etch blocks extracted from `etch-grammar.md` (and `etch-reference-part*.md` blocks tagged `<!-- ebnf-example -->`) parse without error; a doc example without parser support fails CI.
- Re-bench reference machines (Win11 + Fedora 44) for S3/S4/S5, plus incremental-compile non-regression.
- **Review E7:** all C0.2 acceptance criteria.

## Out-of-scope

- **Hybrid LR(1) + Pratt parser** — Phase 1 / C1.11 (`etch-parser.md §23`). The parser stays recursive-descent + Pratt.
- **Async codegen to Zig** (state-machine / CPS lowering of `async` / `await` / `race` / `sync` / `branch` / `spawn`). Zig 0.16 has no language-level `async`, so this needs a hand-emitted state machine + task runtime; the spec wants that desugaring factored through the HIR (`etch-ast-ir.md §5.3, §9.4, §11`), and the M0.8 codegen is the direct AST→Zig path with no HIR. Phase 2 per `etch-ast-ir.md §11`. Async is parsed (E2/E3) and interpreted (E3) only.
- **Generic monomorphisation codegen** — same root cause as async (HIR-dependent lowering not present in the direct AST→Zig path). Phase 2 per `etch-ast-ir.md §11`. Generics are parsed and interpreted (E2) only.
- **VM bytecode** (`.etchc`) — Phase 2 (`etch-bytecode.md`).
- **Shader backend codegen** (SPIR-V / MSL / DXIL / WGSL via AIR) — Phase 2+. E6 delivers shader parse + resolver shader-mode validation only.
- **Highlighting / folding / structural-selection visitors** — Phase 2 (`etch-parser.md §14`).
- **Full recovery cascade** (virtual tokens, panic-mode, fine sync points) — Phase 1 / S2+. E1 delivers top-level resync only.
- **Separate CST** — Phase 1 / S0 (`etch-parser.md §10.1`).
- **Runtime execution / wiring of any Level B construct against a real Tier 1 module** (Cortex, UI/Islandz, Kinesis, Pulse, Ember, Sequencer, Render). Level B is declaration + IR only in Phase 0.

## Acceptance criteria

### Tests

- `tests/etch/v0.2/*.etch` — every v0.6 construct parses without error.
- `tests/etch/recovery_toplevel_test.zig` — `test "top-level resync surfaces one diagnostic per broken construct and keeps sane constructs"` — 3 constructs / 1 broken → ≥1 diagnostic per broken construct, the 2 sane constructs appear in the returned AST.
- `tests/etch/ast_stable_interface.zig` — compiles and exercises ≥20 distinct public AST entry points.
- `tests/etch/ebnf_examples_test.zig` — parses ≥30 example blocks from `etch-grammar.md` without error; a doc example with no parser support fails CI.
- One dedicated test per debt (12 total): D-S3-trivia, D-S3-doccomment, D-S3-annot-applicability, D-S3-annot-field-access, D-S3-resource-receiver, D-S4-runtime-query, D-S4-runtime-report, D-S4-ecs-bridge-panic, D-S4-or-archetype, D-S4-multifilter, D-S5-etchcook-inproc, D-S5-synth100-proper.
- Per-stage differential test (E1–E7): Level-A stages byte-exact interpreter↔codegen on world state after N ticks; Level-B/C stages on serialized internal IR/descriptor.
- `tests/etch/reference_500_lines.etch` differential — byte-exact on Level-A behaviour; Level-B/C and generic/async fragments validate parse + codegen-compiles only.

### Benchmarks

- Incremental compile (1 line / 1 file change) — **< 2 s** on the reference machines (S5 non-regression; **sensitive zone** — S5 margin was ~1.9×, extended codegen erodes it; monitor in CI).
- Cold compile (100 `.etch` files) — **< 30 s** (S5 non-regression).
- `reference_500_lines.etch` parse — **< 50 ms**.
- Interpreter hot-reload (edit → AST swap) — **< 500 ms**.
- Re-bench S3/S4/S5 metrics on Win11 + Fedora 44 (reference machines), archived.

### Observable behavior

- A command that parses, interprets, and codegen-compiles `reference_500_lines.etch`, runs both backends N ticks, and reports the byte-exact diff result (Level A) plus the codegen-compiles-clean result (Level B/C).
- A hot-reload demo: edit a rule body in a running interpreter session, observe the AST swap and behaviour change under 500 ms.
- CI log showing `ebnf_examples_test.zig` count of parsed blocks (≥30).

### CI

- `zig build` clean, zero warning, on the configured matrix (`{ubuntu-24.04, windows-2025} × {Debug, ReleaseSafe}`).
- `zig build test` green (Debug + ReleaseSafe). All inline tests reachable from the test root (`engine-zig-conventions.md §13` module rooting — no silently-skipped tests).
- `zig fmt --check` green (generated Zig under `.weld/generated/` excluded per existing config).
- `zig build lint` green.
- `commit-msg` hook green on every commit of the branch.
- Language compliance: `grep` on the diff before PR confirms 100% English in all repo artifacts (code, comments, identifiers, commit messages, branch, brief).

## Notes

- **A/B/C levels rationale.** Level B constructs depend on real Tier 1 modules (Cortex, UI/Islandz, Kinesis, Pulse, Ember, Sequencer, Render), which are out of Phase 0 scope beyond their interface stub. They are therefore declaration + internal IR/descriptor only in this milestone; their runtime wiring is post-Phase-0. This is deliberate, not a gap.
- **Async + generics codegen deferral.** Both are HIR-dependent lowerings (async = state machine / CPS; generics = monomorphisation) that the spec factors through the HIR (`etch-ast-ir.md §5.3, §11`). The M0.8 codegen is the direct AST→Zig path delivered at S5, with no HIR. Both are parsed and interpreted in M0.8; their Zig codegen is Phase 2 per `etch-ast-ir.md §11`. This is the boundary of the plan's "implementable shipping-side" codegen clause.
- **Sensitive criterion.** Incremental compile < 2 s had ~1.9× margin at S5 (the only tight Phase −1 gate). Extended codegen risks eroding it; treat it as a monitored gate, not a safe non-regression.
- **Scope clarification (Claude.ai decision relayed by Guy, 2026-06-06).** for-in + collection indexing/slicing confirmed in E1 as the entailed consumers of the scoped ranges/collections; not a scope expansion. Rationale: a collection without indexing/slicing is unusable, an `Iterator` without for-in is unusable, a range without for-in is inert — so `for ... in`, `arr[i]`, and `arr[0..3]` are not new scope but the consumers the scoped `ranges`/`collections` lines already entail. They are implemented together so each feature has a real differential (`for i in 0..10 { sum += i }`, `arr[i]`, `arr[0..3]`). Anti-hallucination: these forms are implemented strictly from `etch-grammar.md` (`for_stmt` §line 621, `range_expr` §410-411, `postfix_op "[" expression "]"` §425) + `etch-reference-part1.md` (§6.5 ranges, §7.3 for-in, §11 collections) — verified present before writing.
- **Two documentation drifts (out of scope here; patch at M0.8 close, not mid-milestone).** (1) `engine-phase-0-plan.md §M0.8` says "11 debts" but lists 12 (5×D-S3 + 5×D-S4 + 2×D-S5) — it is **12**; the "11" is a typo. (2) `etch-ast-ir.md §11` uses the pre-renumbering Phase 0.5/1/2 labels and an HIR-centric model out of sync with the direct AST→Zig codegen delivered at S5; the relative ordering (async/generics codegen after the basics) is correct, only the phase labels are stale. Both are KB realignments for the plan-close patch (delete-and-reupload), neither touches M0.8 code.

- 2026-06-06 — E1 debt **D-S3-annot-field-access** landed (commit `d4a9895`). The positional-ident path of `parseAnnotationArg` routed the argument through `continuePostfixAndBinary` (binary operators only), so an argument like `@requires(self.health)` lost its `.field` / `.get` postfix chain. Extracted `continuePostfix` from `parsePostfix` and ran it before `continuePostfixAndBinary` on that path, so an annotation argument now accepts the full postfix chain (field access, method get) ahead of any binary continuation. Inline parser test added.

- 2026-06-06 — E1 debts **D-S3-doccomment** + **D-S3-trivia** landed (commit `700ec91`). Lexer: `skipLineComment` distinguishes `///` doc comments (exactly three slashes, not four) from ordinary `//` line comments, bucketing spans into `doc_comment_spans` vs `comment_spans`. AST: `AstArena` gained a `doc_comment_spans` pool plus `leading_comments` / `doc_comments` maps (`AutoHashMapUnmanaged(NodeId, SpanRange)`) with `docCommentsOf` / `leadingCommentsOf` accessors. A free `attachTrivia(arena, gpa)` pass runs at the end of `parse` — two-cursor byte-range bucketing of comment + doc spans onto the top-level item that follows them; intra-item and trailing trivia is left unattached (finer node-level attachment is a Phase 2 concern, per brief scope). Inline lexer + parser tests added.

- 2026-06-06 — E1 debt **D-S3-annot-applicability** landed (commit `2ccbdc3`). New `AnnotTarget` enum + `annotationAppliesTo(kind, target)` predicate + `TypeChecker.validateAnnotations(start, len, target)`, invoked in pass 1 per item (component/resource/rule) and per field (`validateFieldsInDecl`). A builtin annotation on the wrong target now emits **E0502 AnnotationMisapplied**; `.custom` (plugin) annotations are accepted on any target (their schema validation is Phase 3, not this debt). Argument-schema checks (E0503/E0504) are a separate §13 concern, out of scope. Applicability table implemented (**flagged for the Review E1 gate audit** against `etch-resolver-types.md §13.2` + `etch-reference-part3.md` Part III):
  - `@custom` → any target
  - `@save` → component, resource
  - `@requires`, `@storage` → component
  - `@config`, `@state` → resource (confirmed by RTTI `Lifecycle`, `src/core/rtti/type_info.zig`)
  - `@transient` → resource, field
  - `@phase`, `@priority`, `@run_on`, `@pause_group`, `@id` → rule
  - `@unit`, `@range`, `@hidden`, `@readonly`, `@replicated` → field
  - `@networked`, `@loc` → no valid E1 target (their constructs — event, expression — arrive in later stages)

  Boundary calls least certain against the spec prose, to verify at the gate before sign-off: `@id` target (rule vs component), `@networked` (no-target vs component/field), `@save` (component+resource — does it also cover fields?). Fixtures: `components/annotated.etch` corrected (`@replicated` moved onto a field; `@networked` component dropped — no valid E1 target); new invalid fixture `tests/etch/corpus/invalid/E0502_annotation_misapplied.etch` (`@config` on a component) registered in the corpus facade. Inline `types.zig` test asserts misapplied → E0502 plus a fully-valid control asserting no E0502. E0301/E0302 (resource/component-given) are deliberately deferred to the D-S3-resource-receiver commit where they are first used. `zig build test` green (exit 0), `zig fmt --check` clean. Remaining E1: D-S3-resource-receiver (5th debt), expr/control-flow foundations, EBNF harness, Review E1 tests.

- 2026-06-06 — E1 debt **D-S3-resource-receiver** landed (commit `98631c9`) — the 5th and last S3 debt. Receiver-less `get(R)` / `get_mut(R)` is now the resource counterpart of `entity.get(T)`. **Parse:** `parsePrimary` accepts the `get` / `get_mut` keywords at the head of a primary (`parseResourceGetCall`), stored as a `method_get` / `method_get_mut` expr with `receiver == NodeId.none` — no new ExprKind, the absent receiver is the discriminator everywhere downstream. **Resolve:** the bare form resolves `T` as a resource (E0301 ResourceExpectedComponentGiven if `T` is a component) and requires it in the when clause (reusing E1213); the receiver form now rejects a resource `T` with E0302 ComponentExpectedResourceGiven. E0301/E0302 added to the `DiagnosticCode` catalogue (the additive reservation deferred from the annot-applicability commit). `isAssignTargetReachable` needed no change — it already dispatches on `.method_get_mut` vs `.method_get`, identical for both forms, so `get_mut(R).field = x` is allowed and `get(R).field = x` rejected. **Interpret:** new `Value.resource_ref { resource_id, mutable }`; `field_access` read and `execAssign` write dispatch on it through the bridge `readResourceField` / `writeResourceField` (already present, previously unused). **Codegen — deferred, flagged for the gate:** receiver-less get emission is rejected with `UnsupportedConstruct` rather than emitted. Reason: the resource store backs each resource with a byte-aligned `gpa.dupe(u8, …)` buffer, so the natural `@as(*R, @ptrCast(@alignCast(…)))` lowering would trip the `@alignCast` safety check in ReleaseSafe (the CI build mode). Sound codegen needs either aligned resource storage (a core-ECS change, outside this frontend "lexer/parser/resolver core" debt) or byte-level field emission; it folds into the E3 "Level A complete in codegen" gate (brief §Scope E3). `walkExprForComponents` was still fixed to exclude resources from the query tuple. The interpreter is the reference execution and fully exercises the feature. **Tests:** `types.zig` (E0301 / E0302 / E1213 + a valid `get_mut` control), `interp.zig` (a rule reads `get(Score).base` and writes `get_mut(Score).points += …`, asserted end-to-end over 3 ticks), and 3 corpus fixtures (`valid/rules/resource_access.etch`, `invalid/E0301_*`, `invalid/E0302_*`). `zig build test` green (exit 0), `zig fmt --check` clean. **All 5 S3 debts now done.** Remaining E1: expr/control-flow foundations (match/closures/loops/ranges/collections/casts/error-handling/type-alias × parse+interpret+codegen — with `kw_type` added to the `recoverToTopLevel` stop-set IN LOCKSTEP + a recovery test when `type` aliases land), EBNF harness, Review E1 tests.

Type-check: numeric-scalar → numeric-scalar only (E0200). Interpret: int↔float domain flip. Codegen: `@as(T, @floatFromInt/@intFromFloat/@floatCast/@intCast(...))`. Differential program `21_cast_int_to_float` proves interpreter↔cooked-Zig parity (the established per-construct validation pattern: inline types + interp tests, plus a parity program when codegen is in scope). Remaining foundations: ranges, type aliases (with the `recoverToTopLevel` lockstep), loop/labeled/break-value, closures, match (Maranget), collections, error handling. Then EBNF harness + Review E1 tests.

- 2026-06-06 — **Type aliases** landed (commit `9671ab5`) — second foundation, full vertical. `type Name = Type` top-level, resolved through a shared `AstArena.resolveTypeAliasName` chain (cycle-bounded). **Lockstep constraint honored:** `kw_type` joined `parseTopLevel` AND `recoverToTopLevel`'s stop-set together; a dedicated recovery test proves a valid `type` after a broken construct survives in the AST (Guy's E1 forward requirement). Resolve: alias registers as a symbol (E0101 collision), target validated (E0102), substituted at field types / cast targets / `namedTypeToResolved`. Interp: field-kind resolution follows the chain. Codegen: all six field/param/cast/inference sites resolve aliases (the differential program `22_type_alias_field` caught two I'd missed — `emitRegisterCall` + an inference path — proving the value of the interp↔codegen parity check).   **Scope boundaries flagged for the Review E1 gate** (precedent: D-S3-resource-receiver codegen deferral — interp stays the reference execution, codegen defers what needs out-of-path infrastructure):
  - (a) **Heap-collection codegen deferred** — dynamic `T[]`, map, set codegen needs an allocator in the `rule_x(world)` signature + the Etch arena model, outside the direct AST→Zig path. The byte-exact interp↔codegen differential covers **fixed arrays** — exactly the `arr[i]` / `arr[0..3]` / `for-in` consumers the §Notes scope clarification requires.
  - (b) **Collection methods + constructors deferred** — the stdlib API (`.push`/`.len`/`.contains`/…) and `Set.from`/`Set.new` need the **call mechanism** (postfix `.method(args)` / `(args)`), which is E2-adjacent (shared with `fn`/`impl`). Iterator lazy methods (`.map`/`.filter`) additionally need closures (next tranche).
  - (c) **Optional-returning accessors deferred** — `arr.pop()→T?`, `m[k]→V?`, `.get→V?` need `Optional T?`, which is E2. Map access in E1 is via for-in only.
  - (d) **Set runtime deferred** — Set has no literal; its constructors are calls (see b), so E1 ships only the `Set<T>` type (parse + resolve).
  - (e) **Collection field types rejected** — component / resource fields stay scalar POD in E1 (collections are locals).

- 2026-06-06 — **SESSION 2 — closures** landed (7th E1 foundation, commit `3d4e0d0`, diff 29). `|` token; the reserved `closure`/`fn_call` expr kinds are now *produced* (+ closure_exprs/closure_params/call_exprs slabs + helpers). Codegen: capture-free closures → `struct { fn call(params) ret {...} }`, call → `callee.call(args)`.

  - **Capturing-closure codegen deferred** — needs capture analysis + a struct-with-fields lowering (closer to the full closure model); the interp captures by value and is the reference. The byte-exact differential covers capture-free closures (diff 29); an inline interp test covers capture (`scale(5)` capturing `factor` → 15).
  - **Closure body = expression in E1** — a `{ block }` body needs block expressions, which arrive with the loop/break tranche.

- 2026-06-06 — **SESSION 2 — loop / labeled / break-value** landed (8th E1 foundation, commit `6d1bfb2`, diffs 30/31). `loop`/`break`/`continue` keywords (out of `non_s3_keywords`); `loop_expr` added to `ExprKind` (loop is an expression); `break_stmt`/`continue_stmt` produced. Parser: `loop { body }` (§522) + labeled `outer: loop { ... }` (§624, statement position, wrapped as an expr-stmt); `break [label] [value]` (§632), `continue [label]` (§633). The `break [IDENT] [expression]` ambiguity (no statement separator) is resolved by a parser **label stack** — a leading IDENT is a label only when it names an enclosing loop, else it starts the value. Type-check: a loop's value = the type of a top-level break value (permissive). Interp: control-flow via a `self.control` signal (break/continue cross expression↔statement boundaries) + `handleLoopControl`; for-in honours break/continue. Codegen: `loop` → `[label:] while (true) { ... }`, break/continue → Zig (labeled). Diffs 30 (`loop { break 42 }` → 42) + 31 (labeled nested `break outer` → 7).
  **Scope boundary flagged for the Review E1 gate:**
  - **Block expressions + match-statement deferred.** A `{ block }` value-expression and `match` arms whose body is a block enable (a) conditional loop exit (`loop { ...; match c { _ => break } }`) and (b) closure block bodies (`|x| { ... }`). The journal anticipated this ("may entail extending match arms to blocks"). E1 ships the full loop/break/continue mechanism with **break-value + labeled-break** differentials (unconditional breaks); the conditional-exit path (block_expr parse/interp + match-statement if-else codegen) is a coherent refinement deferred to keep the error-handling foundation on schedule. `continue` is parsed + interpreted + lowered, but its differential needs the conditional-exit path (so it is parse-tested only in E1).
  `zig build test` + `zig build lint` + `zig fmt --check` green. Remaining E1: error-handling → EBNF harness → Review E1 tests.

Parser: `throw expression` (§641), `try { ... } catch IDENT { ... }` (§640) — both bodies are statement runs, so no block-expression support is needed. Type-check: the thrown value is typed; the catch binding is `unknown`. Interp: a `self.thrown` signal unwinds (like break/continue) through statement runs / loops / for-in until a `try` clears it and binds the catch var; an uncaught throw at the rule top level is a runtime error.
  **Scope boundaries flagged for the gate:** error-handling codegen deferred (a manual error-union / propagation flag is out-of-path infra, interp is the reference); E1 throws an arbitrary value (the builtin `Error` struct + `ErrorCode` enum arrive with struct/enum in E2); `throws` (fn marker) waits on fn (E2).   1. **Deferred E1 codegen = Level-A codegen completion blocked on E2 primitives, NOT E2 work.** The deferred-codegen paths (heap collections `T[]`/`Map`/`Set`, capturing closures, error-handling, conditional-loop-exit / block-expr / match-statement) are Level-A codegen completion gated on E2 primitives (call mechanism + `Optional` + `Error`/`throws`), not new E2 constructs. Home = the **E3 gate "Level A complete in codegen"**, to be closed once E2 ships those primitives — same fold as the D-S3-resource-receiver codegen deferral (already homed at E3, brief §Scope E3).

Recommendation: **E2 (expression/statement completions)**. To be confirmed at E2 planning — these keywords must not be left in permanent rejection without a decision.
  3. **Heads-up E3 — Tier 0 ECS may need aligned resource storage.** resource-receiver codegen (and heap-collection codegen) may require aligned resource storage on the Tier 0 ECS side (the `@alignCast` ReleaseSafe constraint) — potentially touches the core, not just the Etch frontend.

- 2026-06-07 — **E2 block 1 plan — control-flow completion (EBNF-confirmed before writing, anti-hallucination).** Forms verified in `etch-grammar.md`: `block = "{" {statement} [expression] "}"` (§4.1 l.645 — the trailing expression is the block value); `if_expr`/`if_stmt` = `"if" expression block {"else" "if" expression block} ["else" block]` (§3.2 l.500 / §4.1 l.618); `while_stmt` = `[loop_label] "while" expression block` (§4.1 l.622); `match_arm` = `pattern "=>" (expression | block)` (§3.2 l.504); `block_expr` = `block` as a primary expression (§3.2 l.520). Design:
  - **Trailing-value block:** extend `BlockExpr` with `value: NodeId` (`NodeId.none` = value-less). A new `parseBlock` collects statements; an expression-led item that is not an assignment and is immediately followed by `}` becomes the block value (Etch has no `;`, so "last bare expression before `}`" is the value — reference §6.11/§6.12). Rule/`for`/`while`/`loop`/`try` bodies keep `parseStmtRun` (statement-only, no value extraction) — no regression.
  - **`if`** parsed as an if-expr in `parsePrimary` (mirrors `loop`); in statement position it wraps as an expr-stmt. `while` is statement-only (no while-expr in the EBNF). `match` arms gain block bodies (the deferred match-statement). The `control`/`thrown` signal + `execStmtRun` infra from E1 loop/break already crosses expr↔stmt boundaries, so conditional loop exit (`loop { if c { break } }`) and closure block bodies (`|x| { ... }`) fall out once block-expr + if land — closing the two E1 loop/break deferrals.
  - **`if let` / `while let` deferred to block 5** (Optional dependency: they unwrap `T?`, reference §7.2/§10.1) — a planned within-E2 coupling (deliver with the consumer, as E1 did for ranges + for-in), not a re-scope. Block 1 ships plain `if cond` / `while cond`.
  - Full vertical incl. codegen (Zig has native `if`/`while`/labeled value-blocks) + a differential per construct (Review E1 ruling 2 homes these at E2; launch block 1 = parse + resolve + interp + codegen + differential).

Rule/loop/for/try bodies keep `parseStmtRun` (statement-only). **This single tranche unblocked match-arm block bodies + closure block bodies on the parse + interp side for free** (both route through `parseExpr`).

Closure block body (`|x| { ... }`) lands parse + interp (interpreter test) — codegen deferred to the E3 gate, as for capturing closures.
  **Scope flags for the Review E2 gate:**
  - **`if let` / `while let` deferred to block 5** (Optional dependency, reference §7.2/§10.1) — a planned within-E2 coupling, ships with the Optional primitive.

  **recoverToTopLevel stop-set unchanged** (control-flow constructs are body/expression, not top-level — per ruling 2). EBNF-confirmed before writing: every form verified in `etch-grammar.md §3.2`/`§4.1` (commas required between match arms — caught a missing-comma in a draft differential, fixed per EBNF, not by loosening the parser).

Reco (i) — minimal, mirrors the existing resource form — but the EBNF is the source of truth (not in repo) → needs a Claude.ai ruling/patch, plus the E12xx code.
  - **Mechanism — Tier-0 has the full tick-based machinery, but it is DORMANT.** `World.current_tick` + `World.get_mut(T)` → `arch.markChanged(chunk, col, slot, current_tick)` (writes `changed_tick` + `setDirty`) + `beginFrame` (advances the tick, clears the dirty bitsets) + `archetype.changedTick`/`isChunkClean`/`addedTick` all EXIST (`src/core/ecs/{world,archetype,chunk,change_detection}.zig`). But in the M0.8 runtime: (a) `beginFrame` is never called — `runFor`/`tick` call only `tickBoundary` (resources), so `current_tick` is frozen; (b) the interpreter's component writes (`ecs_bridge.writeComponentField`) AND the codegen's writes (direct `C_arr[slot].field = …`) BYPASS `World.get_mut`, so nothing `markChanged`s; (c) `comptime_query.Row` exposes only component pointers, not `changed_tick`. Options: (A) tick-based per-rule (§5-faithful — advance `current_tick` per tick, route writes through `markChanged`, store per-rule `last_run_tick`, filter `changedTick(slot) > last_run_tick`); (B) dirty-bitset frame-granular (clear per tick, `setDirty` on write, filter `isDirty(slot)` — coarser, most wiring exists). For the M0.8 single-rule-pass-per-tick model (A) and (B) are observationally equivalent; reco (A) (§5-faithful, scheduler-future-proof).
  - **Three wiring points (under reco A), all NEW:** (1) `last_run_tick` per rule — interp: a `RuleDesc.last_run_tick` set after each `runRule`; codegen: a per-rule static; PLUS advancing `current_tick` per tick (call `beginFrame`, or fold the advance into the runtime's tick). (2) `comptime_query` change-tick exposure — `Row` must surface the per-slot `changed_tick` (or the archetype+slot), and the codegen `get_mut` write must `markChanged`. (3) interp dynamic per-component `changed_tick` — the dynamic archetype IS `archetype.Archetype` (so `markChanged`/`changedTick` are available); the gap is the interp's write path calling `markChanged(current_tick)` + tracking `current_tick`/`last_run_tick`. **Load-bearing across both backends: the component WRITE path must mark — today it bypasses the marking entirely, so `Changed` would never fire.** STOP for GO before implementing.

Design, fully worked out + code-verified this session:
     - **TWO structural facts (verified, do not re-derive):** (a) **`comptime_query.Row` exposes only component pointers — NO entity id** (`src/core/ecs/comptime_query.zig` l.52-56). So a tag **mutation** (which needs the entity id for `cmd.setTag`) CANNOT use the comptime-query path → route any rule with a tag mutation through the **arch-walk path** (`emitRuleAsArchWalk`), where `arch.entityIdsConst(chunk)[slot]` is available; emit `const __entity = arch.entityIdsConst(chunk)[slot];` in the slot loop and resolve the body's `entity` ident to it. (b) **The generated `tick(world)` has NO allocator** (l.2342), but a `CommandBuffer` needs `gpa`. → change the generated `tick(world)` → `tick(world, gpa)` AND the differential-harness `Runner.step(self, world)` contract → `step(self, gpa, world)`: `tests/etch_interp/diff_runner.zig` l.134 (`runner.step(gpa, &world)`), `runner_codegen.zig` l.46 (`self.program.tick(world, gpa)`), `runner_interp.zig` l.64 (ignore gpa), plus the two other `tick` callers `src/demo_etch_codegen.zig` l.37 and any `synth_100`/bench caller (re-grep `\.tick(`). `register(world, gpa)` already takes gpa — `tick(world, gpa)` is symmetric.
     - **ALLOCATOR CONTRACT (Guy 2026-06-09, load-bearing — engraved): `tick(world, gpa)` is THE allocator-threading model for ALL of sub-slice C** (heap collections `T[]`/`Map`/`Set`, `string`, `Error` — every generated construct that allocates), not just the tag command buffer. Uniform explicit threading, **no global-allocator magic**. Freezing `tick(world, gpa)` here freezes the C contract — so when implementing it, **confirm the NATURE of the threaded gpa**: it must behave as a **FRAME/TICK allocator reset at the tick boundary**, NOT a persistent gpa that leaks — transient per-tick collection/string allocations (sub-slice C) must be freed at the boundary. **For TAGS this is moot** (the `CommandBuffer` owns + frees its arena each tick via `defer cmd.deinit()` + `flush`'s reset, and `applyTagMutation`'s add-buf is `defer gpa.free`'d), so any gpa is leak-free for the tag codegen → **no STOP for item 1(c)**; thread a plain gpa, the tag path self-manages. But the frame-scoped-vs-persistent choice is the **first half of the sub-slice-C allocator decision, surfaced early via the command_buffer**: when C's heap-allocating constructs land, if establishing the frame-scoped nature is non-trivial → **STOP+surface (Cas 2)**. Do not silently thread a persistent gpa into C's transient allocations.
     - **Uniform tag handling in the arch-walk path** (keeps the comptime-query path untouched; gate-4 monomorphisation count unaffected, as for the existing or/not arch-walk): route a rule through arch-walk when its when-clause has a tag filter OR its body has a tag mutation. `WhenInfo` gains `tag_filters` (op + resolved leaf bits, from a tag table built in `generateFile`). `walkWhen` populates it (+ adds `"TagSet"` to `components` for **positive** ops); `emitArchPredicate` for a positive `.tag_filter` → `arch.hasComponent(TagSet_id)`; the per-slot bit test is a `continue` guard in the arch-walk slot loop reading `TagSet_arr[slot].bits[w] & mask` (ensure `TagSet` in `body_used` so `_arr` is emitted). `emitStmt` `.tag_mutation_stmt` → `cmd.setTag(__entity, world.registry.idOf("TagSet").?, <bit>)` / `clearTag`. `tick` creates `var cmd = CommandBuffer.init(gpa, world); defer cmd.deinit();`, passes `&cmd` to tag-mutating rules (`rule_x(world, cmd)`; non-mutating rules stay `rule_x(world)`), `cmd.flush()` before `world.tickBoundary()`. Emit `TagSet` as `pub const TagSet = extern struct { bits: [N]u64 = [_]u64{0}**N }` (N = `tag_table.words()`) + register via `registerComponentRaw` (size N*8, align 8, no fields) when `leaf_count>0`.
     - **NEGATIVE tag-op codegen DEFERRED → `UnsupportedConstruct`, interp is the reference, flag for Review E3.** `has_no_tag`/`has_no_tags` match entities that may LACK `TagSet`, so the arch-walk `TagSet_arr[slot]` access is undefined for them — the clean cut is to fail loud (the differential uses the positive `has_tag`). The interp already handles negatives.
     - **Differential 43** (`tests/etch_interp/programs/43_*.etch` + sidecar, register in BOTH `corpus_facade.zig` and `codegen_corpus_build.zig`): mirror the interp test — `mark` rule `add_tag`s at tick N (deferred), `count` rule `has_tag`-queries + increments a `Counter` at N+1 → byte-exact (value 2 after 3 ticks). Entities may start WITHOUT `TagSet` (both backends add it via `applyTagMutation` — gpa is available in the interp flush AND the cmd flush). EBNF harness +1 (a tags-with-ops-and-mutation block).

  **Code-verified design (do not re-derive):** the tick model runs each rule to completion in one `stepOnce`; async rules are dispatched by index at their loop position, driven by their `AsyncSlot` (which persists across ticks — the by-value `rd` in `runRule` can't hold state). The cursor is the next top-level body-statement index; a bare top-level `await` statement (an expr-stmt wrapping `.await_expr`) is the only suspension point — `driveAsyncBody` evaluates its target to a `WakeCond`, stores `(cursor=i+1, wake)`, returns; resume re-enters from the cursor with the retained `slot.locals`. The rule-arena store resets (`collections`/`structs`/…) are NOT run for async bodies → POD-only locals (see bounds).

  **Bounds — fail loud, flagged for Review E3 (interp is the reference; deliver less, never silently wrong):**
  - **`async fn` interpretation = T2, not T1.** Function coloring (§9.3) makes an async fn's sole call path `await <async_fn()>` = a Future-await, which is T2. So `async fn` *parses* (E2) but its *invocation* stays fail-loud (`callFn` l.1212). T1's observable surface is `async rule`. (A structural consequence of coloring + the T2 deferral, not an under-delivery.)
  - **await targets:** `wait_unscaled` (needs a real clock, out of E3), `entity_event` (the global EventStore models no per-entity event source — its entity-association is §9-underspecified for M0.8), `future` (T2) → `evalAwaitTarget` RuntimeFailure.
  - **await placement:** nested (in `if`/`loop`/`match`) or value (`let x = await …`) await → `evalExpr`'s `await_expr` arm fail-louds (the path-cursor + value-threading is the Phase-2 state machine; e.g. the §7.5 `loop { await … }` pattern is deferred).
  - **async rule shape:** parameterless + non-entity-bound (a resource-only `when` is fine); an entity-bound / parameterised async rule → fail-loud in `runAsyncRule` (one-task-per-matching-entity lifecycle deferred).
  - **heap locals across suspend:** POD-only — a heap collection/struct local would be wiped by another rule's store reset between suspend and resume (the persistent-heap state struct of `memory-model §5.7` is not modelled at the tree-walk level).
  - **same-tick event semantics:** a task suspends at `await global_event(T)` without consuming a T present the same tick it spawned; it resumes the next tick T is present, and the producer must precede the awaiter in rule order (the single-pass analogue of the observer drain).
  - **resolver:** `await`'s event type is not validated (an unknown event = silent never-wakes); `await_expr` resolves to `.unknown`. Deferred.
  - **`race`/`sync`/`branch`/`spawn`:** reserved-unproduced (still in `non_s3_keywords`), T2/T3.

To emit `get_mut(R).field = v` natively the codegen must form `*R = @ptrCast(@alignCast(bytes.ptr))`, but `@alignCast(*R)` on a 1-byte-aligned `dupe` buffer **panics in ReleaseSafe**. Options:
    - **(A) Align the Tier-0 resource buffer — RECO.** `ResourceStore.addResource` allocates the byte buffer over-aligned (fixed 16-byte = `ChunkAlignment`, ≥ every POD/extern-struct field alignment — max POD align is 8 per abi-zig §3.1). Generated Zig then does `@ptrCast(@alignCast(world.resources.getMutResource(R_id).?.ptr))` → `*R` natively (zero-cost, faithful to abi-zig §3.1 "pointer identity, no serialization"). Interp unchanged (byte-offset access works on an over-aligned buffer); harness unchanged (reads bytes); byte-exact preserved (the resource is an `extern struct`, the same layout the registry/interp use). ~3-line Tier-0 edit + the codegen `register` passes the alignment.     - **(B) Field-by-field byte-level emission — no Tier-0 change.** Generated Zig never forms `*R`; it reads/writes each accessed field at its byte offset via unaligned helpers (`std.mem.bytesToValue`/`writeInt`/`@memcpy`-through-tmp), mirroring the interp `ecs_bridge`. Zero kernel change, but verbose, per-field-type, replicates field-offset logic, and diverges from the ABI pointer-identity model (more byte-exact-drift surface).

  **Delivered exactly the marker's wiring:** resolver `synthBinary` `.add` arm takes `string + string → string` before the numeric checks (`.sub`/`.mul`/`.div`/`.rem` on strings stay errors); interp `Value.string_run: u32` + per-body `run_strings` store (owned `[]u8`, reset at the `execBody` boundary alongside `collections`, freed in `deinit`) + `stringBytes`/`newRunString` helpers + the `.add` intercept in the `.binary` `evalExpr` arm (NOT `binaryArith`) + `.len()` generalized over `string_id`/`string_run`; codegen `tick` mounts `var __frame = std.heap.ArenaAllocator.init(gpa); defer __frame.deinit();` when any rule allocates (reconciled with the `_ = gpa;` no-alloc path and the tag `CommandBuffer` path — gpa backs both), arena-needing rule fns gain the conditional trailing `, fa: std.mem.Allocator` param (after `*CommandBuffer`/`*EventCursor`), the dispatch passes `__frame.allocator()`, `LocalCtx.arena_param` threads the name, and the string `.add` emits `std.mem.concat(fa, u8, &.{ lhs, rhs }) catch unreachable` — the same logical point as the interp intercept.

  **One acted deviation from the marker's sketch (mechanism, not scope):** `ruleNeedsArena` as a body-walk was replaced by an **exact two-pass classification** — each rule fn is first emitted into a scratch buffer with the arena available and `Writer.arena_used` records whether the emission actually allocated; iff set, the scratch (whose signature already carries `fa`) is spliced, else the fn is re-emitted without the param. Rationale (code-verified, not assumed): Zig 0.16 rejects BOTH an unused fn param and a pointless discard, so an over- OR under-approximating walk breaks the generated code on any divergence from the emitter's own string-typing; flag-on-emission cannot diverge by construction. Cost: one throwaway emission per rule (text-only, no compile) — negligible against the S5 gates.

  **Fail-loud guards added with the same change (locked bounds, not scope creep):** string `Eq`/`Ord` comparison and string compound assignment (`s += t`) are now rejected at TYPE-CHECK (deferred per the marker; previously they would have failed divergently — interp `RuntimeFailure` vs invalid generated Zig). Concat inside a top-level `fn`/method body (no arena in scope) stays `CodegenError.UnsupportedConstruct` — the §6.3 outparam-arena model for fns is deferred until a tranche needs string-returning fns.

  **Differential 46** (`46_string_concat`): lit+lit concat (`("ab"+"cd").len() → 4`), ident-lhs concat through a `let` binding, and nested left-associated concat (`(prefix+"cd"+"e").len() → 5`), observables routed through POD component fields. - 2026-06-10 — **E3 sub-slice C — tranche 6 (closures: capturing / block-body / signal boundary) DELIVERED in 4 commits, byte-exact (`6acfc2c` fix returning-boundary, `f4f71df` capturing + E0221 + differential 56, `19f530c` block-body + differential 57, `88dcf2a` throwing boundary + differential 58).** Reprise on `275e238` (clean, up to date); read the tranche-6 RESUME MARKER (l.785); re-read the PATCHED specs: `etch-reference-part1.md` §5.6/§6.13 (capture transparente, the two body forms), `etch-memory-model.md` §5.5/§5.6 (M0.8's surface is the NON-ESCAPING subset — §3.2's `ClosureStruct` ABI is the escaping services frontier, not crossed by any differential, so the tranche-3 internal-shape precedent applies: the local Zig lowering is free as long as the observable state matches at the same logical point), `etch-resolver-types.md` §8 (capture analysis; §8.2 value-by-copy for POD scalars vs ref-by-ref for strings/collections; E0221), `etch-diagnostics.md` §5 (E0221 ClosureCannotMutateCapture catalogued). Every marker anchor re-verified in code before writing: `closureHasCaptures`'s conservative `else => true` (ALL block bodies routed to fail-loud, not just capturing ones), the interp `ClosureStore` creation-time locals snapshot (by-value capture already at the right logical point), `callFn`/`callMethod` boundary-consume, the tranche-2 throws machinery (`try_label`/`throws_fn`/`__err`/`__terr_K`).

  **The E2 forward-note semantics EXECUTED (the marker's ruling, first thing in the tranche):** re-verified the interp closure call path as instructed — the leak was REAL. `.block_expr` eval leaves `returning` set (interp.zig block arm) and the closure `fn_call` path returned the body value without consuming, so a `return` inside a closure aborted the ENCLOSING body. Fixed BEFORE any codegen (`6acfc2c`): the closure call boundary now consumes `returning` exactly like `callFn` (the return value becomes the call's value), while `thrown` and `break`/`continue` keep propagating and the call yields unit — the unified-signal frontier as ratified. Dedicated regression test (return-in-closure → the enclosing rule continues, out = 42; pre-fix the signal leaked and out stayed 0).

  **Emission shapes validated in REAL Zig before writing (anti-hallucination):** a throwaway `zig test` file proved all three forms on 0.16 — and surfaced one structural fact that shaped the design: Zig REFUSES a same-name destructure across the nested-fn boundary ("local constant shadows local variable from outer scope"), so captured names cannot rebind inside the `call` fn. Hence the `.capture` `LocalKind`: captured idents emit as `__self.<name>` through the central ident arm — no destructures, no renames, struct fields keep the Etch names.

  **Delivered, the three stages:** (1) **Capturing codegen (`f4f71df`)** — struct-with-fields (the E1 plan): a scope-aware collector walks the body (params, body-local `let`s, loop vars, if/while-let and catch bindings all bind; first-reference order = field order, deterministic), bounded to POD scalar value locals (`i64`/`f64`/`bool` — the §8.2 value-by-copy rows the codegen records concretely); the closure lowers to `struct { <caps>, fn call(__self: @This(), <params>) … }{ .<cap> = <cap>, … }` — the instantiation reads the outer bindings AT CREATION, the interpreter's exact snapshot point, and `callee.call(args)` serves both the instance (method call) and the capture-free TYPE (namespace call, byte-identical to E1). Resolver gains **E0221** (capture-name set snapshotted at `synthCall` body typing; assignment to a captured ident → E0221 even on a `let mut` source; a body-local `let` releases its name). Differential **56** (`56_closure_capture_value`): source mutated AFTER creation, both backends see the snapshot (2 + 40 = 42; by-ref would give 102). (2) **Block-body codegen (`19f530c`)** — the block's statements emit straight into the `call` fn; an internal `return` is the anonymous fn's own natural Zig boundary (nothing simulates a leak — the codegen image of the interp fix by construction), the trailing value becomes the final `return` (void fn when valueless); the body emission resets `try_label`/`arena_param`/`throws_fn`/`fn_ret_zig` (a NEW fn boundary). Differential **57** (`57_closure_block_return`): `pick(7)` hits the internal `return 40`, the rule continues, out = 42. EBNF harness **47 → 48** (block-body closure with internal return — a new surface form; expression-bodied simple + capturing closures were already blocks 256/265). (3) **Throwing boundary (`88dcf2a`)** — `thrown` PROPAGATES through the closure call (contrary to `returning`, exactly like `callFn`): a throwing-body closure's `call` fn rides its own hidden `__err: *?Error` out-param (the tranche-2 throws-fn machinery verbatim — `throws_fn` ctx inside the body, store + zero-default abort), `LocalInfo.closure_node` lets the call site see the body, and the sanctioned let-initializer call site declares `__terr_K` and re-raises into the enclosing try. `exprCanThrow` gains the `.closure` arm (creation marks the run can-throw — a deliberate over-approximation, documented). Differential **58** (`58_closure_thrown_propagation`): the closure throws inside a try, the catch lands 42 in both backends.

  **Validation:** differentials **01–58** byte-exact; generated p56/p57/p58 eyeball-verified line-for-line (creation-time `.factor = factor` snapshot before the source mutation; `return 40;`/`return x;` inside the emitted fn; `__err.* = Error{ … }` + `__terr_0` + `break :__try_0` re-raise); EBNF harness **48**; new tests: 1 interp regression (return-in-closure), 1 resolver block (E0221: mut-capture rejected / body-local shadow clean / read-capture clean), 5 codegen blocks (capturing shape + namespace control, string-capture fail-loud POD bound, block-body shape, throwing shape + sanctioned call site, unsanctioned expression position fail-loud). Suites green **Debug + ReleaseSafe** (exit 0; known macOS spurious noise) + `zig fmt` + `zig build lint` + hooks.

  **Bounded / flagged:** (a) Captures = POD scalars only (`i64`/`f64`/`bool`); `string`/collection captures are §8.2 REF-captures — deferred fail-loud (interpreter reference), as are struct / `Entity` / component-alias / closure captures. (b) Nested closures, `match`, `emit`, `entity.get(_mut)`, `get(R)` inside a closure body → fail-loud (the collector; ECS world machinery is cross-fn in the emitted shape). (c) Throwing-closure calls are sanctioned at the let-initializer ONLY (narrower than throws fns' let + bare-call pair); expression position fails loud. (d) A throwing closure must be CREATED inside the try that catches it — created outside, the try scan does not see it, the plumbing is elided, and the call's re-raise site fails loud; a created-but-never-called throwing closure inside a try is the documented over-approximation family (loud `zig build` never-mutated-var, never silent). (e) The closure throws-effect is NOT E0902-gated (effect propagation through closure types = Phase 1 `etch-resolver-types.md` §9 work); an uncaught throwing-closure call takes the interp's uncaught-throw runtime-error path. (f) E0221 gates IDENT targets; a field-write through a captured struct local is not gated — struct captures fail loud in codegen anyway, and the interp's struct-handle capture (shared store) is flagged as part of the non-POD-capture family, to close with it. (g) Valueless block-body closures emit a void `call` fn — typed unit by the resolver, no differential uses one.

Three survey findings materially changed the work vs the debt letters:

Typed conversions land where a kind is well-defined: integer `div`/`rem` (DivisionByZero vs IntegerOverflow via operand inspection — `intDiv` nulls on both), checked `add`/`sub`/`mul` overflow, bridge-error mapping at the component/resource read+write sites, uncaught Etch throw (span = the thrown value expression, captured at the raise). **Bound (flagged for review): the residual ~95 untyped raise sites still count in `runtime_errors` and leave `last_error` untouched** — typing them is mechanical follow-up, not gate-blocking. 3 dedicated tests: sync div-zero with byte-span equality on `"1 / d"`, UncaughtThrow payload, async choke point.
  3. **D-S4-runtime-query: the DROP exit (brief-sanctioned), and the trap it exposed is a CI-criterion violation that extended to LIVE modules.** `RuntimeQuery`/`World.query_dynamic` had ZERO consumers repo-wide, and the E3 hot path is structurally inexpressible on its flat includes/excludes (or/not trees, tag predicates, changed baselines, three independent per-slot stages) — routing through it would have meant designing a new abstraction, defeating the debt. Dropped (`a02bc33`): `query_runtime.zig` deleted, `World.query_dynamic` + the ecs-root re-export removed, a comptime `!@hasDecl` guard test pins the drop. **Empirical finding folded into the same commit:** `query_runtime`'s inline tests never ran under `zig build test` (un-pinned module, lazy analysis — proven by `--test-filter` returning "0 tests"), and the SAME trap silently skipped the inline tests of four LIVE modules (`registry` 8, `resources` 5, `comptime_query` 3, `chunk` 5 — 21 tests, violating the brief's "no silently-skipped tests" CI criterion). All four pinned in the core test root; all 21 newly-collected tests pass.
  4. **D-S4-multifilter (`3fdb634`) — worse than engraved: silent last-wins overwrite in BOTH backends.** `lowerWhen`'s `ctx.filter.* = .{…}` (interp) and `walkWhen`'s `filter.* = .{…}` (codegen) both kept only the LAST `has T { f == v }` clause — and because both dropped the SAME filter, the 60-program differential corpus exhibited parity-on-wrong-semantics (invisible to the harness; "the diff corpus is green" is void on this class). Both slots became arrays (the `tag_predicates` pattern): `RuleDesc.field_filters` + flat-AND `allFiltersPass`; `WhenInfo.field_filters` + one continue guard per filter on both emission paths, every filter component folded into `body_used`. **Differential 61** (`61_filter_two_components`, next number 62) pins the fix with the load-bearing quadrant entity (passes the LAST filter, fails the FIRST — wrongly matched pre-fix); generated p61 eyeball-verified (both guards on the comptime-query path).
  5. **D-S4-or-archetype (`4545d88`) — delivered per the M0.8 letter (bitset + count heuristic), interpreter-side.** Above `or_bitset_threshold = 8` (a documented tuning constant — value flagged for review, not a frozen decision) an `or` rule iterates a per-rule `DynamicBitSetUnmanaged` of matching archetype indices, lazily tail-extended by `rescanOrBitset` (the Tier-0 `Query.maybeRescan` pattern; sound: archetype component sets are immutable post-creation, the list is append-only, ascending iteration preserves walk order → differential parity untouched). Below the threshold the direct walk is byte-identical (the whole corpus, 1–3 archetypes). The per-archetype chunk walk factored into `iterateArchetype` (shared by both paths); `RuntimeReport.predicate_archetype_evals` is the proof observable. 2 dedicated tests: above-threshold (full scan tick 1 → 8 evals; steady state → 0; one tail eval + match correctness after a new archetype) and below-threshold control (per-tick re-evaluation preserved). **Scope note for review: interpreter-only per the S4 letter — the codegen arch-walk keeps its static per-program emission (no runtime cache); the asymmetry is flagged, not silently closed.**
  6. **D-S5-etchcook-inproc (`b427635`) — the survey located the COSTLY boundary at the bench, not the build graph.** The S5 letter names the `b.addRunArtifact` boundary, but those two cooks are build-cached (cost only on input change); the real per-run subprocess cost was `bench/etch_compile.zig` spawning `zig-out/bin/etch_cook` 10+ times INSIDE metric (a)'s timed region. Fix: the consolidation pipeline (header, parse/check/codegen per input, import strip, namespace wrap, programs table, Gate-4 stats) moved into the library as `zig_codegen/consolidate.cookConsolidated` over in-memory sources; the CLI is a thin file-I/O shim; the bench imports `weld_etch` and cooks in-process (`runCookInProcess`, same read/cook/write cycle). The build-graph `addRunArtifact` cooks are KEPT (idiomatic, cached; folding the full Etch frontend into the build runner would lengthen the build-runner compile against the <2 s gate). **Metric (a) protocol note (CLAUDE.md baseline safeguard): the first in-process measurement is a NEW baseline — the child-spawn overhead is gone; gates have wide margin (S5: 1104 ms vs 30 s).** Smoke-validated on the 100-file corpus: Gate 4 unchanged (382 distinct signatures / 400 rules). **Bonus latent-bug fix:** the build-graph cooks passed `name=path` as raw strings — Zig hashes string args by bytes, so `.etch` content edits did not invalidate the cached cook; `addPrefixedFileArg` now content-tracks every input. Dedicated test: `tests/etch/cook_consolidate_test.zig` (namespaces, single header, programs table, stats, valid-Zig parse, typed failure).
  7. **D-S5-synth100-proper (`f61c2fd`) — the triangle idiom, measurement path untouched.** `bench/fixtures/synth_100/` is now a real package: `build.zig.zon` path-dep `.weld = .{ .path = "../../.." }`; its `build.zig` cooks the committed corpus through `weld.artifact("etch_cook")` (content-tracked inputs) and compiles the output against `weld.module("weld_core")` into a stub exe (bench-stub shape — `register`/`tick` referenced, never called, so the `tick(world, gpa)` signature needs no wiring). The placeholder's stated blocker (`b.path("../...")` forbidden) is exactly what the dependency mechanism solves (triangle precedent). Proof = the new root `verify-synth-100` step (nested `zig build` + `setCwd`), green locally, wired as a single-leg CI step (ubuntu Debug) — kept OUT of `zig build test` (nested cold build). **The S5 bench's direct `zig build-exe` incantation is deliberately untouched — it is the opposable protocol (the 1.9× incremental margin is the tight gate).**
  All green on every commit: `zig build test` Debug AND ReleaseSafe (exit 0; the known macOS spurious `failed command` noise only), `zig build lint`, `zig fmt --check`, hooks. **Differentials 01–61; EBNF harness 48** (no grammar-surface change in D); `recoverToTopLevel` unchanged.   1. `bt_leaf` is an UNDEFINED nonterminal (`etch-grammar.md` §8.1: `bt_node = bt_composite | bt_leaf`, no `bt_leaf` production). The example's intent (a binding consumed by the NEXT action in the sequence) suggests `let_stmt` belongs in `bt_action`; strict EBNF rejects the canonical example. Ruling requested.

- 2026-06-10 — **EVALUATION-ORDER RULING (Claude.ai, relayed by Guy) — named-argument evaluation is SOURCE ORDER (written order), REPLACING the parameter-order contract recorded in the named-args entry above (E4 extension 2/2). Delivered as a standalone fix; differential 66 REPROVEN with an order observable.**
  **The ruling:** §3.3 pins the positional-then-named mix (conformant) but NOT the evaluation order; parameter order was the wrong contract — call-site side-effect order would depend on the callee's SIGNATURE, so reordering a fn's parameters would silently change the behaviour of every named call site (semantic breakage at a distance). Source order keeps the call site self-contained (effects follow the reading — the Rust/C#/Swift contract).
  **Implementation:** arguments EVALUATE in written order into temporaries, then BIND in parameter order — `callArgIndexForParam` (the index variant of the shared binding) is the one algorithm; only evaluation separates from passing. Interp: `callFn`/`callMethod` evaluate a source-order value buffer then bind per param (receiver still first — written first). Codegen: named calls emit a labeled block (`__na<seq>: { const __na<seq>_<j> = <arg j in source order>; …; break :__na<seq> callee(<temps in parameter order>); }`); the receiver of a named-arg METHOD call is bounded to the PURE shapes (`Type.assoc` path / ident / non-opt field access) so its emitted position after the temps is unobservable — anything else fails loud (recorded bound). Both backends at the same logical point.
  **Proof:** differential 66 reproven — the two named-argument blocks append ordered digits into a local (`order` = **32**, written order; parameter order would read 23) ALONGSIDE the binding observable (out += 23) — byte-exact both backends; the rest of the suite untouched.
